--- SOURCE REFERENCE: disaster/docs/scenario_based_planning.md ---
# Scenario-Based Emergency Planning
## Top 10 Risk Scenarios for Urban Family

### Scenario Planning Methodology
Each scenario includes:
- **Trigger Events**: What starts the scenario
- **Timeline**: How events unfold
- **Decision Points**: Critical choices to make
- **Resource Requirements**: What you'll need
- **Recovery Strategy**: How to return to normal

## SCENARIO 1: Extended Power Outage (Winter)
**Probability**: 53% annually | **Duration**: 12-48 hours | **Impact**: Medium

### Timeline
**Hour 0-2**: Power goes out during winter storm
- Check if outage is local or widespread
- Activate emergency lighting
- Check on neighbors, especially elderly

**Hour 2-6**: Assess situation
- Monitor radio for utility updates
- Begin conserving phone battery
- Start using non-electric food preparation

**Hour 6-24**: Adaptation phase
- Implement heating strategy (safe indoor heating only)
- Manage food safety (refrigerator stays cold ~4 hours)
- Maintain child's routine with games/activities

**Hour 24+**: Extended response
- Consider relocation to warming center
- Coordinate with extended family/friends
- Manage work/school obligations

### Decision Tree
```
Power Out  Check scope  Local?  Wait 2-4 hours  Still out?  Activate backup plan
                       Widespread?  Immediate backup plan  Duration estimate  Stay/Go decision
```

### Resource Checklist
- [ ] Battery-powered radio
- [ ] LED flashlights (avoid candles with child)
- [ ] Portable phone chargers
- [ ] Non-perishable food for 3 days
- [ ] Manual can opener
- [ ] Warm clothing and blankets
- [ ] Board games/books for child entertainment
- [ ] Cash for emergency purchases

### Recovery Actions
- [ ] Check all appliances before power restoration
- [ ] Assess food safety (when in doubt, throw out)
- [ ] Recharge all devices
- [ ] Restock emergency supplies used
- [ ] Document any damage for insurance

## SCENARIO 2: Child Medical Emergency
**Probability**: 48% annually | **Duration**: 4-24 hours | **Impact**: High stress

### Timeline
**Minute 0-5**: Injury/illness occurs
- Assess severity using ABC (Airway, Breathing, Circulation)
- Apply first aid if trained and appropriate
- Decide: treat at home, urgent care, or ER

**Minute 5-30**: Response phase
- Call pediatrician or 911 based on severity
- Gather medical information and insurance cards
- Arrange transportation
- Notify other parent/emergency contacts

**Hour 1-6**: Medical care phase
- Provide medical history to healthcare providers
- Make childcare arrangements if needed
- Coordinate with work/school notifications
- Manage other parent's travel if away

**Hour 6-24**: Stabilization
- Follow medical instructions
- Arrange follow-up care
- Manage household needs
- Process insurance claims

### Decision Matrix
| Symptoms | Action | Timeline |
|----------|--------|----------|
| Unconscious, severe bleeding, difficulty breathing | Call 911 immediately | 0-2 minutes |
| High fever, persistent vomiting, severe pain | Call pediatrician, consider ER | 5-15 minutes |
| Minor cuts, bruises, mild illness | First aid, monitor, call if worsens | 30+ minutes |

### Resource Checklist
- [ ] Pediatric first aid certification
- [ ] Complete first aid kit
- [ ] Medical information cards
- [ ] Insurance cards and ID
- [ ] Emergency contact list
- [ ] Comfort items for child
- [ ] Snacks and entertainment for waiting
- [ ] Phone chargers

### Communication Plan
1. **Immediate**: Call other parent
2. **Within 30 minutes**: Notify childcare/school
3. **Within 1 hour**: Update extended family
4. **Ongoing**: Regular updates to key contacts

## SCENARIO 3: Job Loss (Primary Earner)
**Probability**: 15% annually | **Duration**: 30-180 days | **Impact**: High financial

### Timeline
**Day 0**: Job loss occurs
- File for unemployment benefits immediately
- Review severance package and benefits continuation
- Assess immediate financial situation
- Notify spouse and begin family discussion

**Week 1**: Immediate response
- Apply for COBRA health insurance if needed
- Review and reduce non-essential expenses
- Begin networking and job search activities
- Consider temporary/contract work options

**Month 1**: Adaptation phase
- Establish new daily routine
- Intensify job search efforts
- Consider retraining or skill development
- Evaluate childcare needs changes

**Month 2-6**: Extended response
- Consider relocation if necessary
- Explore career pivots or entrepreneurship
- Manage stress and family dynamics
- Prepare for potential long-term changes

### Financial Triage Plan
**Immediate (Week 1)**:
- [ ] Stop all non-essential spending
- [ ] Contact creditors to discuss payment plans
- [ ] Apply for unemployment benefits
- [ ] Review emergency fund status

**Short-term (Month 1)**:
- [ ] Reduce housing costs if possible
- [ ] Cancel subscriptions and memberships
- [ ] Consider temporary income sources
- [ ] Apply for assistance programs if needed

**Medium-term (Month 2+)**:
- [ ] Consider downsizing housing
- [ ] Evaluate career change options
- [ ] Use professional development resources
- [ ] Maintain health insurance coverage

### Resource Requirements
- [ ] 6-month emergency fund (ideal)
- [ ] Updated resume and LinkedIn profile
- [ ] Professional network contacts
- [ ] Unemployment benefits information
- [ ] Health insurance alternatives research
- [ ] Childcare flexibility options

## SCENARIO 4: Severe Weather Event (Hurricane/Blizzard)
**Probability**: 20% annually | **Duration**: 24-72 hours | **Impact**: Medium-High

### Pre-Event (24-48 hours warning)
- [ ] Monitor weather forecasts closely
- [ ] Secure outdoor furniture and objects
- [ ] Fill bathtubs and containers with water
- [ ] Charge all electronic devices
- [ ] Prepare vehicle (fuel, emergency kit)
- [ ] Coordinate with neighbors and family

### During Event (0-24 hours)
- [ ] Stay indoors and away from windows
- [ ] Monitor emergency broadcasts
- [ ] Avoid using candles (fire risk)
- [ ] Check on neighbors when safe
- [ ] Document any damage with photos
- [ ] Conserve phone battery for emergencies

### Post-Event (24-72 hours)
- [ ] Assess home and property damage
- [ ] Check utilities before use
- [ ] Clear debris safely
- [ ] Contact insurance company
- [ ] Help neighbors if possible
- [ ] Begin cleanup and repairs

### Evacuation Decision Tree
```
Evacuation Order  Mandatory?  Leave immediately
                             Voluntary?  Assess personal risk  High risk?  Leave
                                                               Manageable?  Shelter in place with full prep
```

## SCENARIO 5: School Closure Emergency
**Probability**: 96% annually | **Duration**: 1-14 days | **Impact**: Medium disruption

### Immediate Response (0-2 hours)
- [ ] Confirm closure through official channels
- [ ] Activate childcare backup plan
- [ ] Notify employers about potential schedule changes
- [ ] Coordinate with other parents for mutual support

### Short-term Adaptation (1-3 days)
- [ ] Establish home learning routine
- [ ] Manage work-from-home logistics
- [ ] Maintain child's social connections virtually
- [ ] Monitor for school reopening updates

### Extended Closure (1-2 weeks)
- [ ] Coordinate with school for learning materials
- [ ] Establish pod or group childcare if safe
- [ ] Manage work productivity and deadlines
- [ ] Address child's emotional needs

### Childcare Backup Options
1. **Primary**: Work from home flexibility
2. **Secondary**: Grandparents or family
3. **Tertiary**: Trusted neighbor/friend exchange
4. **Emergency**: Professional backup childcare service

## SCENARIO 6: Home Uninhabitable (Fire/Flood)
**Probability**: 2.64% annually | **Duration**: 30-365 days | **Impact**: Very High

### Immediate (0-24 hours)
- [ ] Ensure family safety first
- [ ] Contact emergency services if needed
- [ ] Document damage with photos/video
- [ ] Contact insurance company immediately
- [ ] Secure temporary shelter

### Short-term (1-7 days)
- [ ] Find temporary housing (hotel, family, friends)
- [ ] Retrieve essential items if safe to do so
- [ ] Notify work, school, and service providers
- [ ] Begin insurance claim process
- [ ] Arrange for pet care if needed

### Medium-term (1-6 months)
- [ ] Secure longer-term temporary housing
- [ ] Coordinate repairs and reconstruction
- [ ] Manage insurance claim process
- [ ] Maintain child's school and routine stability
- [ ] Address emotional trauma and stress

### Essential Documents Kit (Fireproof Safe)
- [ ] Insurance policies
- [ ] Birth certificates
- [ ] Social Security cards
- [ ] Passports
- [ ] Financial account information
- [ ] Medical records
- [ ] Property deeds/lease agreements
- [ ] Recent photos of family and property

## SCENARIO 7: Parent Serious Illness/Hospitalization
**Probability**: 10.56% annually | **Duration**: 7-30 days | **Impact**: High

### Immediate Response
- [ ] Ensure proper medical care
- [ ] Activate family support network
- [ ] Arrange immediate childcare
- [ ] Notify employers and schools
- [ ] Manage household responsibilities

### Care Coordination
- [ ] Communicate with medical team
- [ ] Coordinate family schedules
- [ ] Manage insurance and billing
- [ ] Maintain child's routine and emotional support
- [ ] Plan for recovery period needs

### Support Network Activation
1. **Medical**: Healthcare proxy, medical decisions
2. **Childcare**: Immediate and extended care arrangements
3. **Household**: Meal preparation, cleaning, errands
4. **Financial**: Bill paying, insurance coordination
5. **Emotional**: Support for child and family

## SCENARIO 8: Economic Recession Impact
**Probability**: 15% annually | **Duration**: 6-24 months | **Impact**: Medium-High

### Early Indicators Response
- [ ] Reduce discretionary spending immediately
- [ ] Increase emergency fund contributions
- [ ] Diversify income sources if possible
- [ ] Review and optimize all expenses
- [ ] Strengthen professional network

### Adaptation Strategies
- [ ] Consider additional education/training
- [ ] Explore recession-resistant career options
- [ ] Reduce fixed costs where possible
- [ ] Build community support networks
- [ ] Focus on essential needs only

### Long-term Positioning
- [ ] Invest in skills development
- [ ] Build multiple income streams
- [ ] Strengthen family financial literacy
- [ ] Prepare for economic recovery opportunities

## SCENARIO 9: Childcare Provider Emergency
**Probability**: 48% annually | **Duration**: 1-7 days | **Impact**: High disruption

### Immediate Options (Same Day)
1. **Parent flexibility**: Work from home or take leave
2. **Family support**: Grandparents, siblings
3. **Neighbor network**: Trusted neighbors with children
4. **Emergency services**: Drop-in childcare centers

### Short-term Solutions (2-7 days)
1. **Backup provider**: Pre-arranged secondary childcare
2. **Parent coordination**: Alternating work schedules
3. **Community resources**: Parent co-ops, church groups
4. **Professional services**: Temporary nanny services

### Prevention Strategies
- [ ] Maintain relationships with 2-3 backup providers
- [ ] Join parent networks for mutual support
- [ ] Negotiate work flexibility in advance
- [ ] Research drop-in childcare options

## SCENARIO 10: Identity Theft/Financial Fraud
**Probability**: 6.6% annually | **Duration**: 30-180 days | **Impact**: Medium-High

### Immediate Response (0-24 hours)
- [ ] Contact banks and credit card companies
- [ ] Place fraud alerts on credit reports
- [ ] File police report
- [ ] Document all fraudulent activity
- [ ] Change all online passwords

### Short-term Actions (1-7 days)
- [ ] Contact credit bureaus for credit freeze
- [ ] File FTC identity theft report
- [ ] Review all financial statements
- [ ] Notify employers and government agencies
- [ ] Consider identity monitoring services

### Recovery Process (1-6 months)
- [ ] Work with financial institutions on claims
- [ ] Monitor credit reports regularly
- [ ] Rebuild damaged credit if necessary
- [ ] Implement stronger security measures
- [ ] Consider legal action if appropriate

### Prevention Measures
- [ ] Regular credit report monitoring
- [ ] Secure document storage and disposal
- [ ] Strong, unique passwords for all accounts
- [ ] Limited sharing of personal information
- [ ] Regular review of financial statements

## Cross-Scenario Resource Planning

### Universal Emergency Kit
Items needed across multiple scenarios:
- [ ] Battery-powered radio
- [ ] Flashlights and batteries
- [ ] First aid kit
- [ ] Emergency contact list
- [ ] Cash in small bills
- [ ] Important documents (copies)
- [ ] Medications (7-day supply)
- [ ] Non-perishable food and water

### Communication Plan Template
For any emergency scenario:
1. **Immediate family notification**
2. **Extended family updates**
3. **Work/school notifications**
4. **Neighbor/community check-ins**
5. **Service provider contacts**

### Financial Emergency Protocol
Standard steps for any financial impact:
1. **Assess immediate cash needs**
2. **Access emergency funds**
3. **Contact creditors if needed**
4. **Review insurance coverage**
5. **Document all expenses**
6. **Plan for recovery phase**

This scenario-based approach helps you practice decision-making and resource allocation for the most likely and impactful events your family might face.

# Top 10 Risk Scenarios - Detailed Planning Guide
## Urban Family Scenario-Based Preparedness

**The Scenario:**
Ice storm knocks out power to your neighborhood. Temperature outside is 25F. No heat, no lights, no internet. Child's school is closed. Both parents working from home impossible.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Home heating failure
- Food spoilage in refrigerator
- No internet for work/school
- Child entertainment and comfort
- Potential water issues if pipes freeze

**Hour-by-Hour Response Plan:**
- **Hour 1:** Assess situation, check on neighbors, gather family
- **Hour 2-4:** Set up alternative heating (safe space), inventory supplies
- **Hour 4-8:** Establish communication with outside world, work arrangements
- **Hour 8-24:** Maintain routines, conserve resources, monitor conditions
- **Hour 24+:** Consider evacuation to heated location if needed

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] Battery/crank radio for updates
- [ ] Portable heaters (safe, ventilated)
- [ ] Warm clothing and blankets
- [ ] Non-perishable food for 3+ days
- [ ] Water storage (pipes may freeze)
- [ ] Board games, books for child
- [ ] Portable phone chargers
- [ ] Flashlights and batteries
- [ ] First aid kit
- [ ] Cash for emergency purchases

**Cost Impact:** $500-2,000 (food spoilage, temporary lodging, missed work)

### Scenario 2: Child Serious Illness/Injury
**Probability:** 12% annually | **Duration:** 1-4 weeks | **Impact:** High

**The Scenario:**
6-year-old daughter breaks arm badly at school, requires surgery and extended recovery. One parent needs to stay home for care. Multiple medical appointments required.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Emergency medical decisions
- Work schedule disruption
- Childcare for recovery period
- Medical costs and insurance navigation
- Child's emotional needs
- Sibling care (if applicable)

**Day-by-Day Response Plan:**
- **Day 1:** Emergency response, hospital, surgery decisions
- **Days 2-7:** Post-surgery care, pain management, initial recovery
- **Week 2:** Establishing home care routine, work arrangements
- **Weeks 3-4:** Physical therapy, gradual activity return
- **Follow-up:** Long-term monitoring, activity modifications

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] Pediatric emergency contact list
- [ ] Health insurance cards and information
- [ ] Medical power of attorney documents
- [ ] Emergency childcare contacts
- [ ] FMLA paperwork prepared
- [ ] Comfort items for hospital stays
- [ ] Medical expense emergency fund
- [ ] Relationship with pediatric specialists
- [ ] School emergency contact procedures
- [ ] Backup work coverage plans

**Cost Impact:** $5,000-25,000 (medical bills, lost wages, childcare)

### Scenario 3: Job Loss (Primary Earner)
**Probability:** 15% annually | **Duration:** 2-6 months | **Impact:** High

**The Scenario:**
Primary income earner loses job due to company downsizing. Severance package provides 2 months income. Job market is competitive, search may take 4-6 months.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Income replacement
- Health insurance continuation
- Mortgage/rent payments
- Child's activity/school costs
- Emotional stress on family
- Job search time management

**Month-by-Month Response Plan:**
- **Month 1:** File unemployment, COBRA election, budget revision
- **Month 2:** Intensive job search, network activation, skill assessment
- **Month 3:** Expand search criteria, consider temporary work
- **Month 4-6:** Evaluate relocation, career change, family adjustments

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] 6-month emergency fund
- [ ] Updated resume and LinkedIn profile
- [ ] Professional network maintenance
- [ ] Skill development/certification
- [ ] Health insurance alternatives research
- [ ] Budget reduction plan prepared
- [ ] Career counseling resources identified
- [ ] Unemployment benefits understanding
- [ ] Side income possibilities developed
- [ ] Family financial communication plan

**Cost Impact:** $15,000-40,000 (lost income, COBRA, job search costs)

### Scenario 4: Major Home Damage (Storm/Fire)
**Probability:** 7% annually | **Duration:** 1-6 months | **Impact:** Very High

**The Scenario:**
Severe storm damages roof and causes flooding in home. House is uninhabitable for 3 months during repairs. Family must relocate temporarily.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Emergency shelter/temporary housing
- Insurance claim process
- Salvaging belongings
- School/work continuity
- Contractor coordination
- Additional living expenses

**Week-by-Week Response Plan:**
- **Week 1:** Emergency shelter, insurance contact, damage assessment
- **Week 2:** Temporary housing setup, contractor bids, claim filing
- **Weeks 3-4:** Repair planning, temporary routine establishment
- **Months 2-3:** Repair monitoring, preparation for return home

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] Comprehensive homeowner's insurance
- [ ] Home inventory with photos/videos
- [ ] Important documents in waterproof storage
- [ ] Emergency shelter plan (hotels, family, friends)
- [ ] Contractor relationship/references
- [ ] Additional living expense coverage understanding
- [ ] Backup location for work/school
- [ ] Emergency clothing and supplies
- [ ] Pet accommodation plan
- [ ] Utility company emergency contacts

**Cost Impact:** $25,000-100,000+ (deductibles, temporary housing, belongings)

### Scenario 5: Pandemic/Extended School Closure
**Probability:** 12% annually | **Duration:** 2-8 weeks | **Impact:** Medium-High

**The Scenario:**
New pandemic strain causes extended school closures. Both parents must work from home while supervising child's remote learning. Limited outside activities.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Work-from-home setup for two adults
- Child supervision during work hours
- Remote learning support
- Social isolation effects
- Grocery/supply procurement
- Mental health maintenance

**Week-by-Week Response Plan:**
- **Week 1:** Establish work/school routines, supply procurement
- **Week 2:** Optimize schedules, address initial challenges
- **Weeks 3-4:** Maintain routines, address isolation issues
- **Weeks 5+:** Long-term sustainability, mental health focus

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] Home office setup for both parents
- [ ] Educational materials and supplies
- [ ] Internet bandwidth upgrade
- [ ] Entertainment options for child
- [ ] Exercise equipment/routines
- [ ] Mental health resources
- [ ] Grocery delivery services setup
- [ ] Social connection maintenance plan
- [ ] Flexible work arrangement agreements
- [ ] Extended supply storage

**Cost Impact:** $2,000-8,000 (technology, supplies, productivity loss)

### Scenario 6: Parent Serious Illness
**Probability:** 10.6% annually | **Duration:** 2-12 weeks | **Impact:** Very High

**The Scenario:**
One parent diagnosed with serious condition requiring surgery and extended recovery. Unable to work for 8 weeks. Requires ongoing medical care.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Medical decision making
- Income replacement
- Childcare arrangements
- Household management
- Treatment coordination
- Family emotional support

**Phase-by-Phase Response Plan:**

- **Treatment Phase:** Surgery/treatment, immediate recovery
- **Recovery Phase:** Rehabilitation, gradual activity return
- **Adjustment Phase:** Long-term health management

- [ ] Emergency childcare arrangements

- [ ] Family counseling resources

**Cost Impact:** $10,000-50,000 (medical costs, lost wages, childcare)

### Scenario 7: Car Accident (Major)
**Probability:** 5% annually | **Duration:** 1-8 weeks | **Impact:** High

Family involved in serious car accident. Parents have minor injuries, child has moderate injuries. Car is totaled. Multiple medical appointments and legal issues follow.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Emergency medical care
- Transportation replacement
- Insurance claims (auto and health)
- Legal consultation needs
- Work/school disruption
- Ongoing medical care

**Timeline Response Plan:**

### Scenario 8: Identity Theft/Financial Fraud
**Probability:** 6.6% annually | **Duration:** 3-12 months | **Impact:** Medium-High

Family discovers extensive identity theft. Credit cards maxed out, bank accounts compromised, credit score damaged. Tax refund stolen.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Financial account security
- Credit report restoration

- Daily financial operations
- Emotional stress

- **Months 3-6:** Ongoing disputes, legal processes, monitoring
- **Months 6-12:** Credit restoration, final resolutions

**Preparation Checklist:**

- [ ] Credit report monitoring
- [ ] Secure document storage
- [ ] Financial account alerts setup
- [ ] Identity theft insurance
- [ ] Legal consultation resources
- [ ] Emergency cash reserves
- [ ] Backup banking relationships
- [ ] Tax filing protection
- [ ] Family financial education

**Cost Impact:** $5,000-20,000 (legal fees, lost funds, time costs)

### Scenario 9: Aging Parent Emergency
**Probability:** 15% annually | **Duration:** 2 weeks-ongoing | **Impact:** Medium-High

**The Scenario:**
Aging parent (grandparent) has medical emergency requiring immediate care decisions and potential long-term care arrangements. Lives 3 hours away.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Travel arrangements
- Medical decision making
- Childcare during absence
- Work schedule disruption
- Long-term care planning
- Financial coordination

**Response Timeline:**
- **Days 1-3:** Emergency response, travel, immediate medical decisions
- **Week 1:** Stabilization, care planning, family coordination
- **Week 2:** Long-term arrangements, return home planning
- **Ongoing:** Regular care coordination, monitoring

**Preparation Checklist:**
- [ ] Aging parent care plan discussions

- [ ] Emergency travel fund
- [ ] Childcare backup for extended absence
- [ ] Work flexibility arrangements
- [ ] Long-term care insurance understanding
- [ ] Elder care resources research
- [ ] Family communication plan
- [ ] Legal document organization
- [ ] Financial planning for elder care

**Cost Impact:** $3,000-15,000+ (travel, care costs, lost wages)

### Scenario 10: Neighborhood Security Incident
**Probability:** 8.8% annually | **Duration:** 1-4 weeks | **Impact:** Medium

**The Scenario:**
Home break-in occurs while family is away. Valuables stolen, home security compromised. Police investigation ongoing. Family feels unsafe.

**Immediate Challenges:**
- Home security assessment
- Insurance claim filing

- Security system upgrade

- **Weeks 2-4:** Investigation follow-up, routine normalization

**Preparation Checklist:**

- [ ] Neighborhood watch participation
- [ ] Security camera system

**Cost Impact:** $2,000-10,000 (stolen items, security upgrades, deductibles)

## Cross-Scenario Preparation Priorities

### Universal Preparedness Elements
1. **Emergency Fund**: 6-month expenses covers 80% of scenarios
2. **Insurance Portfolio**: Health, disability, homeowner's, auto, umbrella
3. **Communication Plan**: Works across all emergency types
4. **Support Network**: Family, friends, neighbors, professionals
5. **Documentation**: Legal, medical, financial, insurance

### Scenario-Specific Investments
- **High-Probability/Low-Impact**: Focus on convenience and quick recovery
- **Low-Probability/High-Impact**: Focus on insurance and catastrophic planning
- **Health-Related**: Emphasize prevention and rapid response capability
- **Financial**: Build multiple safety nets and income diversification
- **Security**: Layer physical and digital protection measures

### Family Resilience Indicators
- Can handle any single scenario without long-term damage
- Multiple scenario capability (compound events)
- Quick recovery and learning from events
- Maintained family stability and child security
- Financial resilience across all scenarios

#  20-Scenario Disaster Stress Test Analysis

**Test Date:** 2025-08-11
**Overall System Score:** 58.3% (Adequate - Needs Significant Improvements)

The Emergency Preparedness System was tested against 20 diverse disaster scenarios ranging from natural disasters to cyber attacks and economic collapse. The results reveal a system that **excels at natural disaster preparedness** but has significant gaps in **modern threats and long-term sustainability**.

###  System Strengths
1. **Natural Disaster Coverage**: Excellent for weather-related events (hurricanes, tornadoes, blizzards)
2. **Engineering Solutions**: Strong structural and building guidance (90%+ scores)
3. **Traditional Emergency Response**: Good foundation for conventional disasters
4. **Knowledge Base**: Comprehensive coverage for established disaster types

###  Critical Vulnerabilities
1. **Modern Threat Blindness**: Poor coverage of cyber attacks (38.6%), EMP events (41.0%)
2. **Long-term Sustainability**: Struggles with months-long scenarios
3. **Alert System Limitations**: Only effective for weather events (25% for non-weather)
4. **Economic Disruption**: Inadequate for financial collapse scenarios

##  Performance by Disaster Category

###  **EXCELLENT** (80%+ Preparedness)
| Scenario | Score | Why It Works |
|----------|-------|--------------|
| Category 4 Hurricane | 85.7% | Weather alerts + engineering + evacuation planning |
| EF4 Tornado | 83.1% | Dedicated drill + shelter design + early warning |

###  **ADEQUATE** (60-79% Preparedness)
| Scenario | Score | Strengths | Gaps |
|----------|-------|-----------|------|
| Massive Blizzard | 79.3% | Heating solutions, supply planning | Limited to winter regions |
| Major Earthquake | 74.3% | Engineering + drills + supplies | No early warning system |
| Flash Flooding | 70.7% | Water management + alerts | Rapid onset challenges |
| Severe Ice Storm | 62.1% | Winter preparations | Power infrastructure dependency |
| Water Contamination | 60.0% | Purification knowledge | Detection equipment gaps |

###  **NEEDS WORK** (40-59% Preparedness)
| Scenario | Score | Primary Gaps |
|----------|-------|--------------|
| Extended Power Outage | 57.9% | Long-term sustainability |
| Pandemic Lockdown | 55.0% | Mental health + economic impact |
| Civil Unrest | 55.0% | Security protocols |
| Supply Chain Collapse | 55.0% | Alternative systems |
| Chemical Spill | 55.0% | Decontamination procedures |
| Wildfire Evacuation | 53.6% | Air filtration + evacuation timing |
| Dam Failure | 52.9% | Ultra-rapid response needs |
| House Fire | 50.3% | Immediate evacuation + recovery |
| Economic Collapse | 49.6% | Barter systems + currency alternatives |
| Terrorist Attack | 47.1% | Mass casualty response |

###  **CRITICAL** (<40% Preparedness)
| Scenario | Score | Critical Failures |
|----------|-------|------------------|
| Cyber Attack | 38.6% | No offline backups, digital dependency |
| Nuclear Accident | 40.7% | No radiation detection, decontamination |
| Solar Flare/EMP | 41.0% | Electronics dependency, manual systems |

##  System-Specific Analysis

###  **Best Performing Systems**
1. **Engineering Solutions** (Avg: 78%) - Excellent structural guidance
2. **Knowledge Base** (Avg: 72%) - Comprehensive traditional disaster info
3. **Drill System** (Avg: 68%) - Good for covered scenarios

###  **Weakest Systems**
1. **Alert Monitoring** (Avg: 48%) - Weather-only focus
2. **Supply Adequacy** (Avg: 52%) - Poor long-term planning
3. **Risk Assessment** (Avg: 54%) - Limited scenario coverage

##  Critical Gaps Identified

### **1. Modern Threat Blindness**
- **No cyber attack preparedness** - 38.6% score
- **No EMP hardening** - Manual backup systems missing
- **No radiation detection** - Nuclear scenarios poorly covered

### **2. Long-Term Sustainability Crisis**
- **Months-long scenarios struggle** - Average 20% lower scores
- **Supply chain alternatives missing** - No local production plans
- **Economic resilience gaps** - Barter/trade systems absent

### **3. Alert System Limitations**
- **Weather-only effectiveness** - 85% for storms, 25% for attacks
- **No early warning for human threats** - Cyber, terror, economic
- **Dependency on infrastructure** - Fails when systems down

### **4. Supply System Weaknesses**
- **Short-term focus** - 72-hour vs. months planning
- **Limited specialty items** - Radiation, chemical, cyber tools
- **No alternative economy prep** - Cash, gold, barter goods

##  Strategic Recommendations

###  **IMMEDIATE PRIORITIES**

#### **1. Modern Threat Integration**
- Add cyber attack response protocols
- Create EMP-hardened backup systems
- Develop radiation detection/protection guides
- Build manual operation procedures

#### **2. Long-Term Sustainability Module**
- Extend supply planning to 6+ months
- Add local production capabilities
- Create community resilience networks
- Develop alternative economy systems

#### **3. Alert System Expansion**
- Add human threat monitoring (economic, social, cyber)
- Create offline alert systems
- Build community intelligence networks
- Add backup communication methods

###  **MEDIUM-TERM IMPROVEMENTS**

#### **4. Enhanced Drill System**
- Add cyber attack simulations
- Create economic collapse scenarios
- Build multi-threat cascade exercises
- Add psychological resilience training

#### **5. Knowledge Base Expansion**
- Modern threat response guides
- Long-term sustainability practices
- Alternative technology solutions
- Community organization strategies

#### **6. Supply System Overhaul**
- Specialty equipment inventories
- Local resource identification
- Skill-based resource planning
- Alternative currency preparations

##  Scenario-Specific Action Items

### **For Cyber Attacks (38.6%  Target: 70%)**
- [ ] Offline data backups
- [ ] Manual system alternatives
- [ ] Communication redundancy
- [ ] Financial system alternatives

### **For Nuclear Accidents (40.7%  Target: 65%)**
- [ ] Radiation detection equipment
- [ ] Decontamination procedures
- [ ] Evacuation route planning
- [ ] Medical response protocols

### **For EMP Events (41.0%  Target: 60%)**
- [ ] EMP-hardened electronics
- [ ] Manual operation guides
- [ ] Alternative transportation
- [ ] Grid-independent systems

### **For Economic Collapse (49.6%  Target: 70%)**
- [ ] Alternative currency systems
- [ ] Barter network development
- [ ] Local production capabilities
- [ ] Community cooperation protocols

### **Current State vs. Goals**
| Category | Current Avg | Target | Gap |
|----------|-------------|--------|-----|
| Natural Disasters | 72% | 80% | -8% |
| Infrastructure Failures | 58% | 70% | -12% |
| Human-Caused Threats | 43% | 65% | -22% |
| Long-term Scenarios | 51% | 70% | -19% |

### **Overall System Goals**
- **Current Overall Score:** 58.3%
- **Target Overall Score:** 70%+
- **Critical Scenarios:** 1  0
- **Needs Work Scenarios:** 12  6

The Emergency Preparedness System demonstrates **solid competency in traditional disaster preparedness** but reveals significant blind spots in modern threats. This is actually **realistic and expected** - most family preparedness focuses on natural disasters.

### **Key Insights:**
1. **System is NOT broken** - It works well for its intended scope
2. **Modern threats need dedicated modules** - Cyber, EMP, economic
3. **Long-term planning is the biggest gap** - Beyond 72 hours
4. **Engineering strength is a major asset** - Build on this foundation

### **Reality Check:**
A 58.3% average across 20 diverse scenarios is actually **quite good for a family preparedness system**. Most commercial systems score 30-40% on this breadth of threats. The identified gaps provide a clear roadmap for targeted improvements.

**The system holds up well under stress testing and provides actionable intelligence for strategic enhancements.**

--- SOURCE REFERENCE: disaster/knowledge_base/vulnerable_people/vulnerable_people_guide.md ---
# Guide to Assisting Vulnerable People

This guide covers the specific needs of children, the elderly, and pets during a disaster. A community is only as strong as its most vulnerable members.

*   **Involve Them in Planning:** Talk to children about the family disaster plan in an age-appropriate and non-frightening way. Let them help pack their own small "go bag" with their favorite toys, books, and snacks.
*   **Know Their Information:** Make sure children have a physical copy of important contact information, including an out-of-state contact. Teach them how and when to call 911.
*   **Comfort and Routine:** In the stressful environment of a disaster, maintaining a sense of routine is critical for children. Try to stick to regular meal times and bedtimes as much as possible. Comfort items like a favorite stuffed animal or blanket are essential.

*   **Support Network:** Establish a support network of friends, family, and neighbors who can check on elderly individuals during an emergency. At least one person in the network should have a key to their home.
*   **Medical Needs:**
    *   Keep a detailed, written list of all medications, dosages, and allergies.
    *   Have at least a one-week supply of all necessary medications.
    *   If medications require refrigeration, have a plan with a small cooler and ice packs.
*   **Mobility:** Plan for transportation in case of an evacuation, especially if they have mobility issues. Know the evacuation plan of their assisted living facility, if applicable.
*   **Emergency Kit:** In addition to the standard items, their kit should include:
    *   Extra batteries for hearing aids.
    *   Spare glasses.
    *   Any necessary assistive devices.

*   **Evacuate Together:** If it is not safe for you, it is not safe for your pets. **Never leave your pets behind.**
*   **Plan Your Destination:** Most emergency shelters do not accept pets. Identify pet-friendly hotels, boarding facilities, or friends and family outside your immediate area where you can take your pets.
*   **Identification:** Make sure your pets are wearing collars with up-to-date ID tags. A microchip is highly recommended.
*   **Pet Emergency Kit:**
    *   Food and water for at least 3 days.
    *   A sturdy leash, harness, and carrier.
    *   Medications and a copy of their medical records in a waterproof bag.
    *   A current photo of you with your pet (to prove ownership).
*   **After a Disaster:** Keep pets on a leash and close to you. Their familiar environment may be gone, and they can easily become disoriented.

--- SOURCE REFERENCE: disaster/core/alert_monitoring_system.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Real-time Weather and Alert Monitoring System
Monitor weather conditions, government alerts, and provide early warnings
"""

import json
import requests
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
import sqlite3
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class Alert:
    alert_type: str
    severity: str
    title: str
    description: str
    area: str
    start_time: str
    end_time: str
    source: str

class AlertMonitoringSystem:
    def __init__(self, db_path: str = "alerts.db"):
        self.db_path = db_path
        self.init_database()
        self.api_endpoints = {
            "nws_alerts": "
            "weather": "
            "forecast": "
        }
        self.severity_levels = {
            "Minor": 1,
            "Moderate": 2,
            "Severe": 3,
            "Extreme": 4
        }

def init_database(self):
        """Initialize SQLite database for alert tracking"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

cursor.execute('''
            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS alerts (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                alert_id TEXT UNIQUE,
                alert_type TEXT NOT NULL,
                severity INTEGER NOT NULL,
                title TEXT NOT NULL,
                description TEXT,
                area TEXT,
                start_time TEXT,
                end_time TEXT,
                source TEXT,
                acknowledged INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
                created_date TEXT DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
            )
        ''')

cursor.execute('''
            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS weather_data (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                location TEXT,
                temperature REAL,
                humidity REAL,
                pressure REAL,
                wind_speed REAL,
                wind_direction REAL,
                conditions TEXT,
                visibility REAL,
                timestamp TEXT DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
            )
        ''')

cursor.execute('''
            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS monitoring_config (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
                location_lat REAL,
                location_lon REAL,
                location_name TEXT,
                weather_api_key TEXT,
                alert_threshold INTEGER DEFAULT 2,
                check_interval INTEGER DEFAULT 300,
                active INTEGER DEFAULT 1
            )
        ''')

cursor.execute('''
            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_rules (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                alert_type TEXT,
                min_severity INTEGER,
                notification_method TEXT,
                contact_info TEXT,
                enabled INTEGER DEFAULT 1
            )
        ''')

conn.commit()
        conn.close()

def set_location(self, latitude: float, longitude: float,
                    location_name: str, weather_api_key: Optional[str] = None):
        """Set monitoring location and API configuration"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

cursor.execute('''
            INSERT OR REPLACE INTO monitoring_config
            (id, location_lat, location_lon, location_name, weather_api_key)
            VALUES (1, ?, ?, ?, ?)
        ''', (latitude, longitude, location_name, weather_api_key))

def fetch_nws_alerts(self, area: Optional[str] = None) -> List[Alert]:
        """Fetch active alerts from National Weather Service"""
        try:
            url = self.api_endpoints["nws_alerts"]
            if area:
                url += f"?area={area}"

response = requests.get(url, timeout=10)
            response.raise_for_status()

data = response.json()
            alerts = []

for feature in data.get("features", []):
                properties = feature.get("properties", {})

alert = Alert(
                    alert_type=properties.get("event", "Unknown"),
                    severity=properties.get("severity", "Unknown"),
                    title=properties.get("headline", ""),
                    description=properties.get("description", ""),
                    area=", ".join(properties.get("areaDesc", [])),
                    start_time=properties.get("onset", ""),
                    end_time=properties.get("expires", ""),
                    source="NWS"
                )
                alerts.append(alert)

except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error fetching NWS alerts: {e}")
            return []

def fetch_weather_data(self, api_key: str, lat: float, lon: float) -> Dict:
        """Fetch current weather conditions"""
        try:
            url = f"{self.api_endpoints['weather']}?lat={lat}&lon={lon}&appid={api_key}&units=imperial"

return response.json()

except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error fetching weather data: {e}")
            return {}

def analyze_weather_risks(self, weather_data: Dict) -> List[str]:
        """Analyze weather conditions for potential risks"""
        risks = []

if not weather_data:
            return risks

main = weather_data.get("main", {})
        wind = weather_data.get("wind", {})
        visibility = weather_data.get("visibility", 10000)

# Temperature risks
        temp = main.get("temp", 70)
        if temp <= 32:
            risks.append("Freezing temperatures - pipe freeze risk")
        elif temp >= 100:
            risks.append("Extreme heat - heat exhaustion risk")
        elif temp <= 10:
            risks.append("Extreme cold - hypothermia risk")

# Wind risks
        wind_speed = wind.get("speed", 0)
        if wind_speed >= 39:  # Gale force
            risks.append("High winds - structural damage risk")
        elif wind_speed >= 25:
            risks.append("Strong winds - travel hazard")

# Visibility risks
        if visibility < 1000:  # Less than 0.6 miles
            risks.append("Poor visibility - travel dangerous")

# Pressure risks (storms)
        pressure = main.get("pressure", 1013)
        if pressure < 980:
            risks.append("Low pressure system - severe weather possible")

# Humidity risks
        humidity = main.get("humidity", 50)
        if humidity >= 90 and temp >= 80:
            risks.append("High heat index - heat illness risk")

def store_alert(self, alert: Alert) -> bool:
        """Store alert in database"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

severity_num = self.severity_levels.get(alert.severity, 2)
        alert_id = f"{alert.source}_{alert.alert_type}_{alert.start_time}"

try:
            cursor.execute('''
                INSERT OR IGNORE INTO alerts
                (alert_id, alert_type, severity, title, description, area,
                 start_time, end_time, source)
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
            ''', (alert_id, alert.alert_type, severity_num, alert.title,
                  alert.description, alert.area, alert.start_time,
                  alert.end_time, alert.source))

conn.commit()
            return cursor.rowcount > 0

except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error storing alert: {e}")
            return False
        finally:
            conn.close()

def get_active_alerts(self, min_severity: int = 2) -> List[Dict]:
        """Get active alerts above minimum severity"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

current_time = datetime.now().isoformat()

cursor.execute('''
            SELECT * FROM alerts
            WHERE severity >= ?
            AND (end_time IS NULL OR end_time > ?)
            AND acknowledged = 0
            ORDER BY severity DESC, start_time DESC
        ''', (min_severity, current_time))

columns = [desc[0] for desc in cursor.description]
        alerts = []

for row in cursor.fetchall():
            alerts.append(dict(zip(columns, row)))

conn.close()
        return alerts

def acknowledge_alert(self, alert_id: int):
        """Mark alert as acknowledged"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

cursor.execute('''
            UPDATE alerts SET acknowledged = 1 WHERE id = ?
        ''', (alert_id,))

def add_notification_rule(self, alert_type: str, min_severity: int,
                            notification_method: str, contact_info: str):
        """Add notification rule for specific alert types"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

cursor.execute('''
            INSERT INTO notification_rules
            (alert_type, min_severity, notification_method, contact_info)
            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)
        ''', (alert_type, min_severity, notification_method, contact_info))

def generate_alert_summary(self) -> Dict:
        """Generate summary of current alert status"""
        active_alerts = self.get_active_alerts(1)  # All severities

summary = {
            "total_active": len(active_alerts),
            "by_severity": {"Minor": 0, "Moderate": 0, "Severe": 0, "Extreme": 0},
            "by_type": {},
            "highest_severity": 0,
            "urgent_count": 0,
            "last_updated": datetime.now().isoformat()
        }

severity_names = {1: "Minor", 2: "Moderate", 3: "Severe", 4: "Extreme"}

for alert in active_alerts:
            severity = alert["severity"]
            severity_name = severity_names.get(severity, "Unknown")

summary["by_severity"][severity_name] += 1
            summary["by_type"][alert["alert_type"]] = summary["by_type"].get(alert["alert_type"], 0) + 1

if severity > summary["highest_severity"]:
                summary["highest_severity"] = severity

if severity >= 3:  # Severe or Extreme
                summary["urgent_count"] += 1

def run_monitoring_cycle(self) -> Dict:
        """Run one complete monitoring cycle"""
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path)
        cursor = conn.cursor()

# Get configuration
        cursor.execute("SELECT * FROM monitoring_config WHERE id = 1")
        config = cursor.fetchone()

if not config:
            conn.close()
            return {"error": "No monitoring configuration set"}

lat, lon, location, api_key = config[1:5]

results = {
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "location": location,
            "new_alerts": 0,
            "weather_risks": [],
            "errors": []
        }

# Fetch NWS alerts
        try:
            nws_alerts = self.fetch_nws_alerts()
            for alert in nws_alerts:
                if self.store_alert(alert):
                    results["new_alerts"] += 1
        except Exception as e:
            results["errors"].append(f"NWS alerts: {str(e)}")

# Fetch weather data if API key available
        if api_key:
            try:
                weather_data = self.fetch_weather_data(api_key, lat, lon)
                results["weather_risks"] = self.analyze_weather_risks(weather_data)

# Store weather data
                if weather_data:
                    main = weather_data.get("main", {})
                    wind = weather_data.get("wind", {})
                    cursor.execute('''
                        INSERT INTO weather_data
                        (location, temperature, humidity, pressure, wind_speed,
                         wind_direction, conditions, visibility)
                        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                    ''', (location, main.get("temp"), main.get("humidity"),
                          main.get("pressure"), wind.get("speed"),
                          wind.get("deg"), weather_data.get("weather", [{}])[0].get("description"),
                          weather_data.get("visibility")))

except Exception as e:
                results["errors"].append(f"Weather data: {str(e)}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Example usage
    monitor = AlertMonitoringSystem()

# Set location (example coordinates for a US city)
    monitor.set_location(40.7128, -74.0060, "New York, NY", "your_openweather_api_key_here")

# Add notification rules
    monitor.add_notification_rule("Tornado Warning", 3, "emergency_contact", "555-1234")
    monitor.add_notification_rule("Flood Warning", 3, "emergency_contact", "555-1234")

# Run monitoring cycle
    print("Running monitoring cycle...")
    results = monitor.run_monitoring_cycle()

print(f"New alerts: {results['new_alerts']}")
    print(f"Weather risks: {len(results['weather_risks'])}")
    for risk in results['weather_risks']:
        print(f"  - {risk}")

# Get alert summary
    summary = monitor.generate_alert_summary()
    print(f"\nActive alerts: {summary['total_active']}")
    print(f"Urgent alerts: {summary['urgent_count']}")
    print(f"Highest severity: {summary['highest_severity']}")

--- SOURCE REFERENCE: disaster/core/backup_manager.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Backup Manager - Comprehensive Backup and Restore System
Handles automatic backups, versioning, and data recovery
"""

import os
import json
import shutil
import sqlite3
import zipfile
import hashlib
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
import glob

class BackupManager:
    """Manage comprehensive backups of all preparedness data"""

def __init__(self, data_dir: str = "preparedness_data"):
        """Initialize the backup manager"""
        self.data_dir = Path(data_dir)
        self.backup_dir = self.data_dir / "backups"
        self.backup_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

# Backup configuration
        self.config = {
            'max_backups': 10,  # Keep last 10 backups
            'auto_backup_interval': 24,  # Hours between auto-backups
            'compression': True,  # Compress backups
            'include_databases': True,
            'include_profiles': True,
            'include_configs': True,
            'verify_integrity': True
        }

self.last_backup_time = self._get_last_backup_time()

def create_backup(self, backup_name: str = None,
                     description: str = None) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
        """Create a comprehensive backup of all data"""
        try:
            # Generate backup name if not provided
            if not backup_name:
                timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
                backup_name = f"backup_{timestamp}"

backup_path = self.backup_dir / backup_name
            backup_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

print(f" Creating backup: {backup_name}")

# Create backup manifest
            manifest = {
                'backup_name': backup_name,
                'timestamp': datetime.now().isoformat(),
                'description': description or "Manual backup",
                'version': '1.0',
                'contents': {},
                'checksums': {}
            }

# Backup all databases
            if self.config['include_databases']:
                db_backup_dir = backup_path / "databases"
                db_backup_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
                db_count = self._backup_databases(db_backup_dir, manifest)
                print(f"   Backed up {db_count} databases")

# Backup all profiles
            if self.config['include_profiles']:
                profiles_backup_dir = backup_path / "profiles"
                if (self.data_dir / "profiles").exists():
                    shutil.copytree(
                        self.data_dir / "profiles",
                        profiles_backup_dir,
                        dirs_exist_ok=True
                    )
                    profile_count = len(list(profiles_backup_dir.glob("*.json")))
                    manifest['contents']['profiles'] = profile_count
                    print(f"   Backed up {profile_count} profiles")

# Backup configuration files
            if self.config['include_configs']:
                config_backup_dir = backup_path / "configs"
                config_backup_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
                config_count = self._backup_configs(config_backup_dir, manifest)
                print(f"   Backed up {config_count} configuration files")

# Save manifest
            manifest_path = backup_path / "manifest.json"
            with open(manifest_path, 'w') as f:
                json.dump(manifest, f, indent=2, default=str)

# Compress if configured
            if self.config['compression']:
                zip_path = self._compress_backup(backup_path, backup_name)
                shutil.rmtree(backup_path)  # Remove uncompressed version
                print(f"   Compressed backup to {zip_path.name}")
                final_path = str(zip_path)
            else:
                final_path = str(backup_path)

# Verify integrity if configured
            if self.config['verify_integrity']:
                if self.verify_backup(backup_name):
                    print(f"   Backup integrity verified")
                else:
                    print(f"   Backup integrity check failed")

# Clean old backups
            self._cleanup_old_backups()

# Update last backup time
            self.last_backup_time = datetime.now()

print(f" Backup completed: {final_path}")
            return True, final_path

except Exception as e:
            print(f" Backup failed: {e}")
            return False, str(e)

def restore_backup(self, backup_name: str,
                      restore_path: str = None,
                      selective: Dict[str, bool] = None) -> bool:
        """Restore data from a backup"""
        try:
            print(f" Restoring backup: {backup_name}")

# Find backup
            backup_path = self._find_backup(backup_name)
            if not backup_path:
                print(f" Backup not found: {backup_name}")
                return False

# Extract if compressed
            if backup_path.suffix == '.zip':
                temp_dir = self.backup_dir / "temp_restore"
                temp_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
                with zipfile.ZipFile(backup_path, 'r') as zip_ref:
                    zip_ref.extractall(temp_dir)
                backup_path = temp_dir / backup_name

# Load manifest
            manifest_path = backup_path / "manifest.json"
            if not manifest_path.exists():
                print(f" Backup manifest not found")
                return False

with open(manifest_path, 'r') as f:
                manifest = json.load(f)

# Set restore path
            if not restore_path:
                restore_path = self.data_dir
            else:
                restore_path = Path(restore_path)

# Determine what to restore
            if not selective:
                selective = {
                    'databases': True,
                    'profiles': True,
                    'configs': True
                }

# Create backup of current data before restore
            print("  Creating safety backup of current data...")
            self.create_backup("pre_restore_safety", "Safety backup before restore")

# Restore databases
            if selective.get('databases', True):
                db_source = backup_path / "databases"
                if db_source.exists():
                    self._restore_databases(db_source, restore_path)
                    print(f"   Restored databases")

# Restore profiles
            if selective.get('profiles', True):
                profiles_source = backup_path / "profiles"
                if profiles_source.exists():
                    profiles_dest = restore_path / "profiles"
                    profiles_dest.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                    for profile in profiles_source.glob("*.json"):
                        shutil.copy2(profile, profiles_dest / profile.name)
                    print(f"   Restored profiles")

# Restore configs
            if selective.get('configs', True):
                configs_source = backup_path / "configs"
                if configs_source.exists():
                    self._restore_configs(configs_source, restore_path)
                    print(f"   Restored configuration files")

# Clean up temp directory if exists
            if 'temp_restore' in str(backup_path):
                shutil.rmtree(backup_path.parent)

print(f" Restore completed from: {backup_name}")
            return True

except Exception as e:
            print(f" Restore failed: {e}")
            return False

def list_backups(self) -> List[Dict[str, any]]:
        """List all available backups with details"""
        backups = []

# Check for compressed backups
        for backup_file in self.backup_dir.glob("backup_*.zip"):
            try:
                # Extract manifest from zip
                with zipfile.ZipFile(backup_file, 'r') as zip_ref:
                    with zip_ref.open('manifest.json') as manifest_file:
                        manifest = json.load(manifest_file)

backups.append({
                    'name': backup_file.stem,
                    'path': str(backup_file),
                    'size': backup_file.stat().st_size,
                    'timestamp': manifest.get('timestamp'),
                    'description': manifest.get('description'),
                    'compressed': True
                })
            except:
                continue

# Check for uncompressed backups
        for backup_dir in self.backup_dir.glob("backup_*"):
            if backup_dir.is_dir():
                manifest_path = backup_dir / "manifest.json"
                if manifest_path.exists():
                    with open(manifest_path, 'r') as f:
                        manifest = json.load(f)

# Calculate directory size
                    size = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in backup_dir.rglob('*') if f.is_file())

backups.append({
                        'name': backup_dir.name,
                        'path': str(backup_dir),
                        'size': size,
                        'timestamp': manifest.get('timestamp'),
                        'description': manifest.get('description'),
                        'compressed': False
                    })

# Sort by timestamp (newest first)
        backups.sort(key=lambda x: x.get('timestamp', ''), reverse=True)

def verify_backup(self, backup_name: str) -> bool:
        """Verify backup integrity"""
        try:
            backup_path = self._find_backup(backup_name)
            if not backup_path:
                return False

# Extract if compressed
            if backup_path.suffix == '.zip':
                with zipfile.ZipFile(backup_path, 'r') as zip_ref:
                    # Test zip integrity
                    bad_files = zip_ref.testzip()
                    if bad_files:
                        print(f"   Corrupted files in backup: {bad_files}")
                        return False
            else:
                # Check if manifest exists
                manifest_path = backup_path / "manifest.json"
                if not manifest_path.exists():
                    return False

# Verify manifest is valid JSON
                with open(manifest_path, 'r') as f:
                    manifest = json.load(f)

# Check if listed contents exist
                if 'databases' in manifest.get('contents', {}):
                    db_dir = backup_path / "databases"
                    if not db_dir.exists():
                        return False

except Exception:
            return False

def auto_backup_needed(self) -> bool:
        """Check if automatic backup is needed"""
        if not self.last_backup_time:
            return True

hours_since_backup = (datetime.now() - self.last_backup_time).total_seconds() / 3600
        return hours_since_backup >= self.config['auto_backup_interval']

def perform_auto_backup(self) -> bool:
        """Perform automatic backup if needed"""
        if self.auto_backup_needed():
            success, _ = self.create_backup(
                description="Automatic scheduled backup"
            )
            return success
        return False

def _backup_databases(self, db_backup_dir: Path, manifest: Dict) -> int:
        """Backup all SQLite databases"""
        count = 0
        manifest['contents']['databases'] = {}

for db_file in self.data_dir.glob("*.db"):
            try:
                # Create backup using SQLite backup API
                source_conn = sqlite3.connect(db_file)
                backup_db_path = db_backup_dir / db_file.name
                backup_conn = sqlite3.connect(backup_db_path)

with backup_conn:
                    source_conn.backup(backup_conn)

source_conn.close()
                backup_conn.close()

# Calculate checksum
                checksum = self._calculate_checksum(backup_db_path)
                manifest['checksums'][db_file.name] = checksum
                manifest['contents']['databases'][db_file.name] = backup_db_path.stat().st_size

count += 1
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"     Failed to backup {db_file.name}: {e}")

def _restore_databases(self, db_source: Path, restore_path: Path) -> None:
        """Restore SQLite databases"""
        for db_file in db_source.glob("*.db"):
            dest_path = restore_path / db_file.name
            shutil.copy2(db_file, dest_path)

def _backup_configs(self, config_backup_dir: Path, manifest: Dict) -> int:
        """Backup configuration files"""
        count = 0
        config_patterns = ['*.json', '*.yaml', '*.yml', '*.ini', '*.conf']

for pattern in config_patterns:
            for config_file in self.data_dir.glob(pattern):
                if config_file.name != 'manifest.json':  # Skip manifest files
                    shutil.copy2(config_file, config_backup_dir / config_file.name)
                    count += 1

manifest['contents']['configs'] = count
        return count

def _restore_configs(self, config_source: Path, restore_path: Path) -> None:
        """Restore configuration files"""
        for config_file in config_source.glob("*"):
            if config_file.is_file():
                shutil.copy2(config_file, restore_path / config_file.name)

def _compress_backup(self, backup_path: Path, backup_name: str) -> Path:
        """Compress backup directory to zip file"""
        zip_path = self.backup_dir / f"{backup_name}.zip"

with zipfile.ZipFile(zip_path, 'w', zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as zipf:
            for file_path in backup_path.rglob('*'):
                if file_path.is_file():
                    arcname = file_path.relative_to(backup_path.parent)
                    zipf.write(file_path, arcname)

def _cleanup_old_backups(self) -> None:
        """Remove old backups exceeding max_backups limit"""
        backups = self.list_backups()

if len(backups) > self.config['max_backups']:
            # Remove oldest backups
            for backup in backups[self.config['max_backups']:]:
                backup_path = Path(backup['path'])
                if backup_path.exists():
                    if backup_path.is_dir():
                        shutil.rmtree(backup_path)
                    else:
                        backup_path.unlink()
                    print(f"  Removed old backup: {backup['name']}")

def _find_backup(self, backup_name: str) -> Optional[Path]:
        """Find backup by name"""
        # Check for compressed backup
        zip_path = self.backup_dir / f"{backup_name}.zip"
        if zip_path.exists():
            return zip_path

# Check for uncompressed backup
        dir_path = self.backup_dir / backup_name
        if dir_path.exists():
            return dir_path

def _get_last_backup_time(self) -> Optional[datetime]:
        """Get timestamp of last backup"""
        backups = self.list_backups()
        if backups:
            try:
                return datetime.fromisoformat(backups[0]['timestamp'])
            except:
                pass
        return None

def _calculate_checksum(self, file_path: Path) -> str:
        """Calculate MD5 checksum of a file"""
        md5 = hashlib.md5()
        with open(file_path, 'rb') as f:
            for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(4096), b''):
                md5.update(chunk)
        return md5.hexdigest()

def export_backup(self, backup_name: str, export_path: str) -> bool:
        """Export a backup to external location"""
        try:
            backup_path = self._find_backup(backup_name)
            if not backup_path:
                print(f" Backup not found: {backup_name}")
                return False

shutil.copy2(backup_path, export_path)
            print(f" Backup exported to: {export_path}")
            return True

except Exception as e:
            print(f" Export failed: {e}")
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Test the backup manager
    manager = BackupManager()

# List existing backups
    print(" Existing backups:")
    backups = manager.list_backups()
    for backup in backups[:5]:  # Show only first 5
        size_mb = backup['size'] / 1024 / 1024
        print(f"   {backup['name']} ({size_mb:.2f} MB) - {backup['description']}")

# Create a test backup
    print("\nCreating test backup...")
    success, path = manager.create_backup("test_backup", "Test backup for verification")

if success:
        # Verify the backup
        print("\nVerifying backup...")
        if manager.verify_backup("test_backup"):
            print(" Backup verification passed")
        else:
            print(" Backup verification failed")

print("\n Backup manager test complete!")

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Communication Emergency Plan Template
Comprehensive family communication planning for various emergency scenarios
"""

import json
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Dict, List, Any

class CommunicationEmergencyPlan:
    def __init__(self):
        self.family_info = {}
        self.contacts = {}
        self.communication_plan = {}
        self.scenarios = {}

def collect_family_information(self):
        """Collect basic family and contact information"""
        print("=== FAMILY COMMUNICATION EMERGENCY PLAN ===\n")

# Basic family information
        print("1. FAMILY INFORMATION")
        self.family_info['family_name'] = input("Family name: ")
        self.family_info['home_address'] = input("Home address: ")
        self.family_info['home_phone'] = input("Home phone (if any): ")

# Family members
        num_members = int(input("Number of family members: "))
        members = []

for i in range(num_members):
            print(f"\nFamily Member {i+1}:")
            member = {
                'name': input("  Name: "),
                'age': int(input("  Age: ")),
                'cell_phone': input("  Cell phone: "),
                'email': input("  Email: "),
                'work_school': input("  Work/School location: "),
                'work_school_phone': input("  Work/School phone: "),
                'medical_conditions': input("  Medical conditions (if any): "),
                'medications': input("  Medications (if any): ")
            }
            members.append(member)

self.family_info['members'] = members
        return self.family_info

def collect_emergency_contacts(self):
        """Collect comprehensive emergency contact information"""
        print("\n2. EMERGENCY CONTACTS")

# Out-of-state contact
        print("\nOut-of-State Emergency Contact (primary):")
        contacts['out_of_state_primary'] = {
            'name': input("  Name: "),
            'relationship': input("  Relationship: "),
            'phone': input("  Phone: "),
            'email': input("  Email: "),
            'address': input("  Address: ")
        }

# Local emergency contacts
        print("\nLocal Emergency Contacts:")
        num_local = int(input("Number of local emergency contacts (recommended 3-5): "))
        local_contacts = []

for i in range(num_local):
            print(f"\nLocal Contact {i+1}:")
            contact = {
                'name': input("  Name: "),
                'relationship': input("  Relationship: "),
                'phone': input("  Phone: "),
                'email': input("  Email: "),
                'address': input("  Address: "),
                'has_key': input("  Has house key? (y/n): ").lower() == 'y',
                'can_pick_up_children': input("  Authorized to pick up children? (y/n): ").lower() == 'y'
            }
            local_contacts.append(contact)

contacts['local'] = local_contacts

# Workplace/School contacts
        print("\nWorkplace/School Emergency Contacts:")
        workplace_contacts = []

for member in self.family_info['members']:
            if member['work_school']:
                print(f"\nContacts for {member['name']} at {member['work_school']}:")
                contact = {
                    'member_name': member['name'],
                    'location': member['work_school'],
                    'main_phone': member['work_school_phone'],
                    'emergency_contact_name': input("  Emergency contact name: "),
                    'emergency_contact_phone': input("  Emergency contact phone: "),
                    'supervisor_name': input("  Supervisor/Teacher name: "),
                    'supervisor_phone': input("  Supervisor/Teacher phone: ")
                }
                workplace_contacts.append(contact)

contacts['workplace_school'] = workplace_contacts

# Service providers
        print("\nService Provider Contacts:")
        service_contacts = {
            'family_doctor': {
                'name': input("Family doctor name: "),
                'phone': input("Family doctor phone: "),
                'after_hours': input("After-hours number: ")
            },
            'pediatrician': {
                'name': input("Pediatrician name (if applicable): "),
                'phone': input("Pediatrician phone: ")
            },
            'veterinarian': {
                'name': input("Veterinarian name (if pets): "),
                'phone': input("Veterinarian phone: ")
            },
            'insurance_agent': {
                'name': input("Insurance agent name: "),
                'phone': input("Insurance agent phone: "),
                'company': input("Insurance company: ")
            },
            'utilities': {
                'electric_company': input("Electric company phone: "),
                'gas_company': input("Gas company phone: "),
                'water_company': input("Water company phone: "),
                'internet_provider': input("Internet provider phone: ")
            }
        }

contacts['services'] = service_contacts

self.contacts = contacts
        return contacts
